[std](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/index.html)::[num](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/index.html)

# Struct NonZero Copy item path

1.79.0 · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#112)

``` rust
pub struct NonZero<T>(/* private fields */)
where
    T: ZeroablePrimitive;
```

Expand description

A value that is known not to equal zero.

This enables some memory layout optimization. For example, `Option<NonZero<u32>>` is the same size as `u32`:

``` rust
use core::{num::NonZero};

assert_eq!(size_of::<Option<NonZero<u32>>>(), size_of::<u32>());
```

## Layout

`NonZero<T>` is guaranteed to have the same layout and bit validity as `T` with the exception that the all-zero bit pattern is invalid. `Option<NonZero<T>>` is guaranteed to be compatible with `T`, including in FFI.

Thanks to the [null pointer optimization](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/index.html#representation "mod std::option"), `NonZero<T>` and `Option<NonZero<T>>` are guaranteed to have the same size and alignment:

``` rust
use std::num::NonZero;

assert_eq!(size_of::<NonZero<u32>>(), size_of::<Option<NonZero<u32>>>());
assert_eq!(align_of::<NonZero<u32>>(), align_of::<Option<NonZero<u32>>>());
```

## Note on generic usage

`NonZero<T>` can only be used with some standard library primitive types (such as `u8`, `i32`, and etc.). The type parameter `T` must implement the internal trait [`ZeroablePrimitive`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/trait.ZeroablePrimitive.html "trait std::num::ZeroablePrimitive"), which is currently permanently unstable and cannot be implemented by users. Therefore, you cannot use `NonZero<T>` with your own types, nor can you implement traits for all `NonZero<T>`, only for concrete types.

1.53.0 (const: 1.53.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the number of leading zeros in the binary representation of `self`.

On many architectures, this function can perform better than `leading_zeros()` on the underlying integer type, as special handling of zero can be avoided.

##### Examples

``` rust
let n = NonZero::<u8>::new(u8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(n.leading_zeros(), 0);
```

1.53.0 (const: 1.53.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the number of trailing zeros in the binary representation of `self`.

On many architectures, this function can perform better than `trailing_zeros()` on the underlying integer type, as special handling of zero can be avoided.

##### Examples

``` rust
let n = NonZero::<u8>::new(0b0101000)?;

assert_eq!(n.trailing_zeros(), 3);
```

1.97.0 (const: 1.97.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns `self` with only the most significant bit set.

##### Example

``` rust
let a = NonZero::<u8>::new(0b_01100100)?;
let b = NonZero::<u8>::new(0b_01000000)?;

assert_eq!(a.isolate_highest_one(), b);
```

1.97.0 (const: 1.97.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the index of the highest bit set to one in `self`.

Note that this is equivalent to [`ilog2`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#method.ilog2 "method std::num::NonZero::ilog2").

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::new(0b1)?.highest_one(), 0);
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::new(0b1_0000)?.highest_one(), 4);
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::new(0b1_1111)?.highest_one(), 4);
```

1.97.0 (const: 1.97.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::new(0b1_0000)?.lowest_one(), 4);
```

``` rust
assert_eq!(b.count_ones(), NonZero::new(3)?);
```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x82u8)?;
let m = NonZero::new(0xa)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_left(2), m);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the right by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the beginning of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `>>` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0xau8)?;
let m = NonZero::new(0x82)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_right(2), m);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the byte order of the integer.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12u8)?;
let m = n.swap_bytes();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x12)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12u8)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroU8;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Au8)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU8::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU8::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from little endian to the target’s endianness.

On little endian this is a no-op. On big endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u8)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u8)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), one.checked_add(1));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_add(1));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Return [`NonZero::<u8>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u8)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u8)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(two, one.saturating_add(1));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_add(1));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self + rhs > u8::MAX`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let one = NonZero::new(1u8)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u8)?;

assert_eq!(two, unsafe { one.unchecked_add(1) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the smallest power of two greater than or equal to `self`. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if the next power of two is greater than the type’s maximum value. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2u8)?;
let three = NonZero::new(3u8)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u8)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), two.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(Some(four), three.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_next_power_of_two() );
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the base 2 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`u8::ilog2`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u8.html#method.ilog2 "method u8::ilog2"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

Note that this is equivalent to [`highest_one`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#method.highest_one "method std::num::NonZero::highest_one").

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(7u8)?.ilog2(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(8u8)?.ilog2(), 3);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(9u8)?.ilog2(), 3);
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the base 10 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`u8::ilog10`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u8.html#method.ilog10 "method u8::ilog10"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(99u8)?.ilog10(), 1);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(100u8)?.ilog10(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(101u8)?.ilog10(), 2);
```

1.85.0 (const: 1.85.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Calculates the midpoint (average) between `self` and `rhs`.

`midpoint(a, b)` is `(a + b) >> 1` as if it were performed in a sufficiently-large signed integral type. This implies that the result is always rounded towards negative infinity and that no overflow will ever occur.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u8)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u8)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u8)?;

assert_eq!(one.midpoint(four), two);
assert_eq!(four.midpoint(one), two);
```

1.59.0 (const: 1.59.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns `true` if and only if `self == (1 << k)` for some `k`.

On many architectures, this function can perform better than `is_power_of_two()` on the underlying integer type, as special handling of zero can be avoided.

##### Examples

``` rust
let eight = NonZero::new(8u8)?;
assert!(eight.is_power_of_two());
let ten = NonZero::new(10u8)?;
assert!(!ten.is_power_of_two());
```

1.84.0 (const: 1.84.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the square root of the number, rounded down.

##### Examples

``` rust
let ten = NonZero::new(10u8)?;
let three = NonZero::new(3u8)?;

assert_eq!(ten.isqrt(), three);
```

1.87.0 (const: 1.87.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the bit pattern of `self` reinterpreted as a signed integer of the same size.

##### Examples

``` rust
let n = NonZero::<u8>::MAX;

assert_eq!(n.cast_signed(), NonZero::new(-1i8).unwrap());
```

1.97.0 (const: 1.97.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Returns the minimum number of bits required to represent `self`.

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::new(0b1)?.bit_width(), NonZero::new(1)?);
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::new(0b111)?.bit_width(), NonZero::new(3)?);
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::new(0b1110)?.bit_width(), NonZero::new(4)?);
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2u8)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u8)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(four), two.checked_mul(two));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_mul(two));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Return [`NonZero::<u8>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2u8)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u8)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(four, two.saturating_mul(two));
assert_eq!(max, four.saturating_mul(max));
```

Multiplies two non-zero integers together, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self * rhs > u8::MAX`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Raises non-zero value to an integer power. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3u8)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27u8)?;
let half_max = NonZero::new(u8::MAX / 2)?;

assert_eq!(Some(twenty_seven), three.checked_pow(3));
assert_eq!(None, half_max.checked_pow(3));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3u8)?;

let max = NonZero::new(u8::MAX)?;
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u8>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u8>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

1.98.0 (const: 1.98.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Parses a non-zero integer from a string slice with digits in a given base.

The string is expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u8>::from_str_radix("A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
assert!(NonZero::<u8>::from_str_radix("1 ", 10).is_err());
```

1.92.0 (const: 1.92.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

Calculates the quotient of `self` and `rhs`, rounding the result towards positive infinity.

The result is guaranteed to be non-zero.

##### Examples


🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust

let m = NonZero::new(0x3a)?;

```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x1234u16)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroU16;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Au16)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU16::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU16::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u16)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u16)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u16::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), one.checked_add(1));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_add(1));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Return [`NonZero::<u16>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u16)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u16)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u16::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(two, one.saturating_add(1));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_add(1));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self + rhs > u16::MAX`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let one = NonZero::new(1u16)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u16)?;

assert_eq!(two, unsafe { one.unchecked_add(1) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

Returns the smallest power of two greater than or equal to `self`. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if the next power of two is greater than the type’s maximum value. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2u16)?;
let three = NonZero::new(3u16)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u16)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u16::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), two.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(Some(four), three.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_next_power_of_two() );
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

Returns the base 2 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`u16::ilog2`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u16.html#method.ilog2 "method u16::ilog2"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

Note that this is equivalent to [`highest_one`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#method.highest_one "method std::num::NonZero::highest_one").

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(7u16)?.ilog2(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(8u16)?.ilog2(), 3);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(9u16)?.ilog2(), 3);
```

1.85.0 (const: 1.85.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

Calculates the midpoint (average) between `self` and `rhs`.

`midpoint(a, b)` is `(a + b) >> 1` as if it were performed in a sufficiently-large signed integral type. This implies that the result is always rounded towards negative infinity and that no overflow will ever occur.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u16)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u16)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u16)?;

assert_eq!(one.midpoint(four), two);
assert_eq!(four.midpoint(one), two);
```

1.59.0 (const: 1.59.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

On many architectures, this function can perform better than `is_power_of_two()` on the underlying integer type, as special handling of zero can be avoided.

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u16>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u16>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2421-2431)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u16>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u16>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x10000b3u32)?;
let m = NonZero::new(0xb301)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_left(8), m);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `>>` shifting operator!

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12345678u32)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x1e6a2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroU32;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Au32)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU32::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU32::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u32)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u32)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u32::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), one.checked_add(1));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_add(1));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Return [`NonZero::<u32>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u32)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u32)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u32::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(two, one.saturating_add(1));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_add(1));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self + rhs > u32::MAX`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let one = NonZero::new(1u32)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u32)?;

assert_eq!(two, unsafe { one.unchecked_add(1) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

Returns the smallest power of two greater than or equal to `self`. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if the next power of two is greater than the type’s maximum value. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2u32)?;
let three = NonZero::new(3u32)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u32)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u32::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), two.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(Some(four), three.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_next_power_of_two() );
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

Returns the base 2 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`u32::ilog2`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u32.html#method.ilog2 "method u32::ilog2"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

Note that this is equivalent to [`highest_one`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#method.highest_one "method std::num::NonZero::highest_one").

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(7u32)?.ilog2(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(8u32)?.ilog2(), 3);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(9u32)?.ilog2(), 3);
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

This is the same operation as [`u32::ilog10`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u32.html#method.ilog10 "method u32::ilog10"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

##### Examples

1.85.0 (const: 1.85.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

Calculates the midpoint (average) between `self` and `rhs`.

`midpoint(a, b)` is `(a + b) >> 1` as if it were performed in a sufficiently-large signed integral type. This implies that the result is always rounded towards negative infinity and that no overflow will ever occur.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u32)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u32)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u32)?;

assert_eq!(one.midpoint(four), two);
assert_eq!(four.midpoint(one), two);
```

1.59.0 (const: 1.59.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u32>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u32>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2433-2443)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u32>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u32>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u64>::new(0b1)?.highest_one(), 0);
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u64>::new(0b1_0000)?.highest_one(), 4);
assert_eq!(NonZero::<u64>::new(0b1_1111)?.highest_one(), 4);
```

1.97.0 (const: 1.97.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

Returns the index of the lowest bit set to one in `self`.

##### Examples

``` rust

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u64>::new(0b1_0000)?.lowest_one(), 4);
```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0xaa00000000006e1u64)?;
let m = NonZero::new(0x6e10aa)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_left(12), m);
```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x1234567890123456u64)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x6a2c48091e6a2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroU64;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Au64)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU64::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU64::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u64)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u64)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u64::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), one.checked_add(1));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_add(1));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Return [`NonZero::<u64>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u64)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u64)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u64::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(two, one.saturating_add(1));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_add(1));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self + rhs > u64::MAX`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let one = NonZero::new(1u64)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u64)?;

assert_eq!(two, unsafe { one.unchecked_add(1) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

Returns the smallest power of two greater than or equal to `self`. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if the next power of two is greater than the type’s maximum value. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2u64)?;
let three = NonZero::new(3u64)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u64)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u64::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), two.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(Some(four), three.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_next_power_of_two() );
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

Returns the base 2 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`u64::ilog2`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u64.html#method.ilog2 "method u64::ilog2"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

Note that this is equivalent to [`highest_one`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#method.highest_one "method std::num::NonZero::highest_one").

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(7u64)?.ilog2(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(8u64)?.ilog2(), 3);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(9u64)?.ilog2(), 3);
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

This is the same operation as [`u64::ilog10`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u64.html#method.ilog10 "method u64::ilog10"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

##### Examples


1.85.0 (const: 1.85.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

Calculates the midpoint (average) between `self` and `rhs`.

`midpoint(a, b)` is `(a + b) >> 1` as if it were performed in a sufficiently-large signed integral type. This implies that the result is always rounded towards negative infinity and that no overflow will ever occur.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u64)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u64)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u64)?;

assert_eq!(one.midpoint(four), two);
assert_eq!(four.midpoint(one), two);
```

1.59.0 (const: 1.59.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

Returns `true` if and only if `self == (1 << k)` for some `k`.

On many architectures, this function can perform better than `is_power_of_two()` on the underlying integer type, as special handling of zero can be avoided.

##### Examples

``` rust
let eight = NonZero::new(8u64)?;

let ten = NonZero::new(10u64)?;
assert!(!ten.is_power_of_two());
```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u64>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u64>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2445-2455)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u64>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u64>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```


[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the right by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the beginning of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `>>` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x4f7613f4u128)?;
let m = NonZero::new(0x13f40000000000000000000000004f76)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_right(16), m);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the byte order of the integer.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12345678901234567890123456789012u128)?;
let m = n.swap_bytes();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x12907856341290785634129078563412)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12345678901234567890123456789012u128)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x48091e6a2c48091e6a2c48091e6a2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroU128;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Au128)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU128::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroU128::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u128)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u128)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u128::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), one.checked_add(1));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_add(1));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Return [`NonZero::<u128>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u128)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u128)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u128::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(two, one.saturating_add(1));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_add(1));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self + rhs > u128::MAX`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let one = NonZero::new(1u128)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u128)?;

assert_eq!(two, unsafe { one.unchecked_add(1) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

Returns the smallest power of two greater than or equal to `self`. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if the next power of two is greater than the type’s maximum value. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2u128)?;
let three = NonZero::new(3u128)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u128)?;
let max = NonZero::new(u128::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), two.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(Some(four), three.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_next_power_of_two() );
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

Returns the base 2 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`u128::ilog2`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u128.html#method.ilog2 "method u128::ilog2"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

Note that this is equivalent to [`highest_one`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#method.highest_one "method std::num::NonZero::highest_one").

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(7u128)?.ilog2(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(8u128)?.ilog2(), 3);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(9u128)?.ilog2(), 3);
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

Returns the base 10 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`u128::ilog10`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u128.html#method.ilog10 "method u128::ilog10"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(99u128)?.ilog10(), 1);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(100u128)?.ilog10(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(101u128)?.ilog10(), 2);
```

1.85.0 (const: 1.85.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

Calculates the midpoint (average) between `self` and `rhs`.

`midpoint(a, b)` is `(a + b) >> 1` as if it were performed in a sufficiently-large signed integral type. This implies that the result is always rounded towards negative infinity and that no overflow will ever occur.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1u128)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2u128)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4u128)?;

assert_eq!(one.midpoint(four), two);
assert_eq!(four.midpoint(one), two);
```

1.59.0 (const: 1.59.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

Returns `true` if and only if `self == (1 << k)` for some `k`.

On many architectures, this function can perform better than `is_power_of_two()` on the underlying integer type, as special handling of zero can be avoided.

##### Examples

``` rust
let eight = NonZero::new(8u128)?;
assert!(eight.is_power_of_two());
let ten = NonZero::new(10u128)?;
assert!(!ten.is_power_of_two());
```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u128>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u128>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2457-2467)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<u128>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<u128>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroUsize;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Ausize)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroUsize::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroUsize::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

``` rust
}
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1usize)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2usize)?;
let max = NonZero::new(usize::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), one.checked_add(1));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_add(1));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value. Return [`NonZero::<usize>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1usize)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2usize)?;
let max = NonZero::new(usize::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(two, one.saturating_add(1));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_add(1));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Adds an unsigned integer to a non-zero value, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self + rhs > usize::MAX`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let one = NonZero::new(1usize)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2usize)?;

assert_eq!(two, unsafe { one.unchecked_add(1) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Returns the smallest power of two greater than or equal to `self`. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if the next power of two is greater than the type’s maximum value. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2usize)?;
let three = NonZero::new(3usize)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4usize)?;
let max = NonZero::new(usize::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(two), two.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(Some(four), three.checked_next_power_of_two() );
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_next_power_of_two() );
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Returns the base 2 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`usize::ilog2`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.usize.html#method.ilog2 "method usize::ilog2"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

Note that this is equivalent to [`highest_one`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#method.highest_one "method std::num::NonZero::highest_one").

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(7usize)?.ilog2(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(8usize)?.ilog2(), 3);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(9usize)?.ilog2(), 3);
```

1.67.0 (const: 1.67.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Returns the base 10 logarithm of the number, rounded down.

This is the same operation as [`usize::ilog10`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.usize.html#method.ilog10 "method usize::ilog10"), except that it has no failure cases to worry about since this value can never be zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(99usize)?.ilog10(), 1);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(100usize)?.ilog10(), 2);
assert_eq!(NonZero::new(101usize)?.ilog10(), 2);
```

1.85.0 (const: 1.85.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Calculates the midpoint (average) between `self` and `rhs`.

`midpoint(a, b)` is `(a + b) >> 1` as if it were performed in a sufficiently-large signed integral type. This implies that the result is always rounded towards negative infinity and that no overflow will ever occur.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1usize)?;
let two = NonZero::new(2usize)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4usize)?;

assert_eq!(one.midpoint(four), two);
assert_eq!(four.midpoint(one), two);
```

1.59.0 (const: 1.59.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Returns `true` if and only if `self == (1 << k)` for some `k`.

On many architectures, this function can perform better than `is_power_of_two()` on the underlying integer type, as special handling of zero can be avoided.

##### Examples

``` rust
let eight = NonZero::new(8usize)?;
assert!(eight.is_power_of_two());
let ten = NonZero::new(10usize)?;
assert!(!ten.is_power_of_two());
```

1.84.0 (const: 1.84.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Returns the square root of the number, rounded down.

##### Examples

``` rust
let ten = NonZero::new(10usize)?;
let three = NonZero::new(3usize)?;

assert_eq!(ten.isqrt(), three);
```

1.87.0 (const: 1.87.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Returns the bit pattern of `self` reinterpreted as a signed integer of the same size.

##### Examples

``` rust
let n = NonZero::<usize>::MAX;

assert_eq!(n.cast_signed(), NonZero::new(-1isize).unwrap());
```

1.97.0 (const: 1.97.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Returns the minimum number of bits required to represent `self`.

##### Examples

``` rust

assert_eq!(NonZero::<usize>::new(0b111)?.bit_width(), NonZero::new(3)?);
assert_eq!(NonZero::<usize>::new(0b1110)?.bit_width(), NonZero::new(4)?);
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

##### Examples


🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<usize>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<usize>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<usize>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<usize>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

1.92.0 (const: 1.92.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2496-2506)

Calculates the quotient of `self` and `rhs`, rounding the result towards positive infinity.

The result is guaranteed to be non-zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let one = NonZero::new(1usize).unwrap();
let max = NonZero::new(usize::MAX).unwrap();
assert_eq!(one.div_ceil(max), one);

let two = NonZero::new(2usize).unwrap();
let three = NonZero::new(3usize).unwrap();
assert_eq!(three.div_ceil(two), two);
```

1.67.0 · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

The size of this non-zero integer type in bits.

This value is equal to [`i8::BITS`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i8.html#associatedconstant.BITS "associated constant i8::BITS").

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<i8>::BITS, i8::BITS);
```

1.70.0 · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

The smallest value that can be represented by this non-zero integer type, equal to [`i8::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i8.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant i8::MIN").

Note: While most integer types are defined for every whole number between `MIN` and `MAX`, signed non-zero integers are a special case. They have a “gap” at 0.

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<i8>::MIN.get(), i8::MIN);
```

1.70.0 · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(-0x7ei8)?;
let m = NonZero::new(0xa)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_left(2), m);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Shifts the bits to the right by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the beginning of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `>>` shifting operator!

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]

let m = NonZero::new(-0x7e)?;
```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the byte order of the integer.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12i8)?;
let m = n.swap_bytes();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x12)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12i8)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroI8;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Ai8)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI8::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI8::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Converts `self` to big endian from the target’s endianness.

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Ai8)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(n.to_be(), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(n.to_be(), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts `self` to little endian from the target’s endianness.

On little endian this is a no-op. On big endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Ai8)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "little") {
    assert_eq!(n.to_le(), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(n.to_le(), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Computes the absolute value of self. See [`i8::abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i8.html#method.abs "method i8::abs") for documentation on overflow behavior.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i8)?;
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Checked absolute value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if `self == NonZero::<i8>::MIN`. The result cannot be zero.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i8)?;
let neg = NonZero::new(-1i8)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i8::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(Some(pos), neg.checked_abs());
assert_eq!(None, min.checked_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Computes the absolute value of self, with overflow information, see [`i8::overflowing_abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i8.html#method.overflowing_abs "method i8::overflowing_abs").

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i8)?;
let neg = NonZero::new(-1i8)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i8::MIN)?;

assert_eq!((pos, false), pos.overflowing_abs());
assert_eq!((pos, false), neg.overflowing_abs());
assert_eq!((min, true), min.overflowing_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Saturating absolute value, see [`i8::saturating_abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i8.html#method.saturating_abs "method i8::saturating_abs").

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i8)?;
let neg = NonZero::new(-1i8)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i8::MIN)?;
let min_plus = NonZero::new(i8::MIN + 1)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(pos, pos.saturating_abs());
assert_eq!(pos, neg.saturating_abs());
assert_eq!(max, min.saturating_abs());
assert_eq!(max, min_plus.saturating_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Computes the absolute value of self without any wrapping or panicking.

##### Example

``` rust
let u_pos = NonZero::new(1u8)?;

assert_eq!(u_pos, i_neg.unsigned_abs());
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Negates self, overflowing if this is equal to the minimum value.

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Saturating negation. Computes `-self`, returning [`NonZero::<i8>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") if `self == NonZero::<i8>::MIN` instead of overflowing.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5i8)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5i8)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i8::MIN)?;
let min_plus_one = NonZero::new(i8::MIN + 1)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.saturating_neg(), neg_five);
assert_eq!(min.saturating_neg(), max);
assert_eq!(max.saturating_neg(), min_plus_one);
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Wrapping (modular) negation. Computes `-self`, wrapping around at the boundary of the type.

See [`i8::wrapping_neg`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i8.html#method.wrapping_neg "method i8::wrapping_neg") for documentation on overflow behavior.

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i8)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i8)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(four), two.checked_mul(two));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_mul(two));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Return [`NonZero::<i8>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i8)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i8)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i8::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(four, two.saturating_mul(two));
assert_eq!(max, four.saturating_mul(max));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Multiplies two non-zero integers together, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self * rhs > i8::MAX`, or `self * rhs < i8::MIN`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let two = NonZero::new(2i8)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i8)?;

assert_eq!(four, unsafe { two.unchecked_mul(two) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

Raises non-zero value to an integer power. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i8)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i8)?;
let half_max = NonZero::new(i8::MAX / 2)?;

assert_eq!(Some(twenty_seven), three.checked_pow(3));
assert_eq!(None, half_max.checked_pow(3));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

##### Examples

``` rust
let max = NonZero::new(i8::MAX)?;
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i8>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i8>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2508-2518)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i8>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i8>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

1.70.0 · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

The smallest value that can be represented by this non-zero integer type, equal to [`i16::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i16.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant i16::MIN").

Note: While most integer types are defined for every whole number between `MIN` and `MAX`, signed non-zero integers are a special case. They have a “gap” at 0.

##### Examples


🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(-0x5ffdi16)?;
let m = NonZero::new(0x3a)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_left(4), m);
```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the byte order of the integer.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x1234i16)?;
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x1234i16)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroI16;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Ai16)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI16::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI16::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Computes the absolute value of self. See [`i16::abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i16.html#method.abs "method i16::abs") for documentation on overflow behavior.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i16)?;
let neg = NonZero::new(-1i16)?;

assert_eq!(pos, pos.abs());
assert_eq!(pos, neg.abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Checked absolute value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if `self == NonZero::<i16>::MIN`. The result cannot be zero.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i16)?;
let neg = NonZero::new(-1i16)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(Some(pos), neg.checked_abs());
assert_eq!(None, min.checked_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Computes the absolute value of self, with overflow information, see [`i16::overflowing_abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i16.html#method.overflowing_abs "method i16::overflowing_abs").

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i16)?;
let neg = NonZero::new(-1i16)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;

assert_eq!((pos, false), pos.overflowing_abs());
assert_eq!((pos, false), neg.overflowing_abs());
assert_eq!((min, true), min.overflowing_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Saturating absolute value, see [`i16::saturating_abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i16.html#method.saturating_abs "method i16::saturating_abs").

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i16)?;
let neg = NonZero::new(-1i16)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;
let min_plus = NonZero::new(i16::MIN + 1)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i16::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(pos, pos.saturating_abs());
assert_eq!(pos, neg.saturating_abs());
assert_eq!(max, min.saturating_abs());
assert_eq!(max, min_plus.saturating_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Wrapping absolute value, see [`i16::wrapping_abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i16.html#method.wrapping_abs "method i16::wrapping_abs").

##### Example

``` rust
let pos = NonZero::new(1i16)?;
let neg = NonZero::new(-1i16)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(pos, pos.wrapping_abs());
assert_eq!(pos, neg.wrapping_abs());
assert_eq!(min, min.wrapping_abs());
assert_eq!(max, (-max).wrapping_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Computes the absolute value of self without any wrapping or panicking.

##### Example

``` rust
let u_pos = NonZero::new(1u16)?;
let i_pos = NonZero::new(1i16)?;
let i_neg = NonZero::new(-1i16)?;
let i_min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;
let u_max = NonZero::new(u16::MAX / 2 + 1)?;

assert_eq!(u_pos, i_pos.unsigned_abs());
assert_eq!(u_pos, i_neg.unsigned_abs());
assert_eq!(u_max, i_min.unsigned_abs());
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Checked negation. Computes `-self`, returning `None` if `self == NonZero::<i16>::MIN`.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5i16)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5i16)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.checked_neg(), Some(neg_five));
assert_eq!(min.checked_neg(), None);
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Negates self, overflowing if this is equal to the minimum value.

See [`i16::overflowing_neg`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i16.html#method.overflowing_neg "method i16::overflowing_neg") for documentation on overflow behavior.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5i16)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5i16)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.overflowing_neg(), (neg_five, false));
assert_eq!(min.overflowing_neg(), (min, true));
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Saturating negation. Computes `-self`, returning [`NonZero::<i16>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") if `self == NonZero::<i16>::MIN` instead of overflowing.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5i16)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5i16)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;
let min_plus_one = NonZero::new(i16::MIN + 1)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i16::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.saturating_neg(), neg_five);
assert_eq!(min.saturating_neg(), max);
assert_eq!(max.saturating_neg(), min_plus_one);
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Wrapping (modular) negation. Computes `-self`, wrapping around at the boundary of the type.

See [`i16::wrapping_neg`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i16.html#method.wrapping_neg "method i16::wrapping_neg") for documentation on overflow behavior.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5i16)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5i16)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i16::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.wrapping_neg(), neg_five);
assert_eq!(min.wrapping_neg(), min);
```

1.87.0 (const: 1.87.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)
