1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i16)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i16)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i16::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(four), two.checked_mul(two));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_mul(two));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Return [`NonZero::<i16>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i16)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i16)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i16::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(four, two.saturating_mul(two));
assert_eq!(max, four.saturating_mul(max));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Multiplies two non-zero integers together, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self * rhs > i16::MAX`, or `self * rhs < i16::MIN`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let two = NonZero::new(2i16)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i16)?;

assert_eq!(four, unsafe { two.unchecked_mul(two) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Raises non-zero value to an integer power. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i16)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i16)?;
let half_max = NonZero::new(i16::MAX / 2)?;

assert_eq!(Some(twenty_seven), three.checked_pow(3));
assert_eq!(None, half_max.checked_pow(3));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Raise non-zero value to an integer power. Return [`NonZero::<i16>::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MIN") or [`NonZero::<i16>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i16)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i16)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i16::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(twenty_seven, three.saturating_pow(3));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_pow(3));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i16>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i16>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i16>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i16>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

1.98.0 (const: 1.98.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2520-2530)

Parses a non-zero integer from a string slice with digits in a given base.

The string is expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

1.70.0 · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

The smallest value that can be represented by this non-zero integer type, equal to [`i32::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i32.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant i32::MIN").

Note: While most integer types are defined for every whole number between `MIN` and `MAX`, signed non-zero integers are a special case. They have a “gap” at 0.

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<i32>::MIN.get(), i32::MIN);
```

1.70.0 · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

1.53.0 (const: 1.53.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Returns the number of leading zeros in the binary representation of `self`.

On many architectures, this function can perform better than `leading_zeros()` on the underlying integer type, as special handling of zero can be avoided.

##### Examples

``` rust
let n = NonZero::<i32>::new(-1i32)?;

assert_eq!(n.leading_zeros(), 0);
```

##### Examples

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x10000b3i32)?;
let m = NonZero::new(0xb301)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_left(8), m);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the byte order of the integer.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12345678i32)?;
let m = n.swap_bytes();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x78563412)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12345678i32)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x1e6a2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroI32;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Ai32)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI32::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI32::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Checked absolute value. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") if `self == NonZero::<i32>::MIN`. The result cannot be zero.

``` rust
let min = NonZero::new(i32::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(Some(pos), neg.checked_abs());
assert_eq!(None, min.checked_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Negates self, overflowing if this is equal to the minimum value.

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Saturating negation. Computes `-self`, returning [`NonZero::<i32>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") if `self == NonZero::<i32>::MIN` instead of overflowing.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5i32)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5i32)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i32::MIN)?;
let min_plus_one = NonZero::new(i32::MIN + 1)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i32::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.saturating_neg(), neg_five);
assert_eq!(min.saturating_neg(), max);
assert_eq!(max.saturating_neg(), min_plus_one);
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Wrapping (modular) negation. Computes `-self`, wrapping around at the boundary of the type.

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i32)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i32)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i32::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(four), two.checked_mul(two));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_mul(two));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Return [`NonZero::<i32>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i32)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i32)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i32::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(four, two.saturating_mul(two));
assert_eq!(max, four.saturating_mul(max));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Multiplies two non-zero integers together, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self * rhs > i32::MAX`, or `self * rhs < i32::MIN`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let two = NonZero::new(2i32)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i32)?;

assert_eq!(four, unsafe { two.unchecked_mul(two) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Raises non-zero value to an integer power. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i32)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i32)?;
let half_max = NonZero::new(i32::MAX / 2)?;

assert_eq!(Some(twenty_seven), three.checked_pow(3));
assert_eq!(None, half_max.checked_pow(3));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

Raise non-zero value to an integer power. Return [`NonZero::<i32>::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MIN") or [`NonZero::<i32>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i32)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i32)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i32::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(twenty_seven, three.saturating_pow(3));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_pow(3));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i32>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i32>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2532-2542)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i32>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i32>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

The smallest value that can be represented by this non-zero integer type, equal to [`i64::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i64.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant i64::MIN").

Note: While most integer types are defined for every whole number between `MIN` and `MAX`, signed non-zero integers are a special case. They have a “gap” at 0.

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]

let m = NonZero::new(0x6e10aa)?;

assert_eq!(n.rotate_left(12), m);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x1234567890123456i64)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x6a2c48091e6a2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroI64;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Ai64)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI64::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI64::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

Negates self, overflowing if this is equal to the minimum value.

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

Saturating negation. Computes `-self`, returning [`NonZero::<i64>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") if `self == NonZero::<i64>::MIN` instead of overflowing.

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i64)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i64)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i64::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(four), two.checked_mul(two));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_mul(two));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Return [`NonZero::<i64>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i64)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i64)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i64::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(four, two.saturating_mul(two));
assert_eq!(max, four.saturating_mul(max));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Multiplies two non-zero integers together, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self * rhs > i64::MAX`, or `self * rhs < i64::MIN`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let two = NonZero::new(2i64)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i64)?;

assert_eq!(four, unsafe { two.unchecked_mul(two) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

Raises non-zero value to an integer power. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i64)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i64)?;
let half_max = NonZero::new(i64::MAX / 2)?;

assert_eq!(Some(twenty_seven), three.checked_pow(3));
assert_eq!(None, half_max.checked_pow(3));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

Raise non-zero value to an integer power. Return [`NonZero::<i64>::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MIN") or [`NonZero::<i64>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i64)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i64)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i64::MAX)?;

```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i64>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i64>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2544-2554)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i64>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i64>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

- `0-9`

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x12345678901234567890123456789012i128)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x48091e6a2c48091e6a2c48091e6a2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroI128;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Ai128)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI128::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroI128::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

Saturating absolute value, see [`i128::saturating_abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i128.html#method.saturating_abs "method i128::saturating_abs").

##### Example

``` rust

assert_eq!(max, min_plus.saturating_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

Wrapping absolute value, see [`i128::wrapping_abs`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i128.html#method.wrapping_abs "method i128::wrapping_abs").

##### Example

``` rust

let neg = NonZero::new(-1i128)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i128::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(pos, pos.wrapping_abs());
assert_eq!(pos, neg.wrapping_abs());
assert_eq!(min, min.wrapping_abs());
assert_eq!(max, (-max).wrapping_abs());
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

Negates self, overflowing if this is equal to the minimum value.

See [`i128::overflowing_neg`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i128.html#method.overflowing_neg "method i128::overflowing_neg") for documentation on overflow behavior.

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

Saturating negation. Computes `-self`, returning [`NonZero::<i128>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") if `self == NonZero::<i128>::MIN` instead of overflowing.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5i128)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5i128)?;
let min = NonZero::new(i128::MIN)?;
let min_plus_one = NonZero::new(i128::MIN + 1)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i128::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.saturating_neg(), neg_five);
assert_eq!(min.saturating_neg(), max);
assert_eq!(max.saturating_neg(), min_plus_one);
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i128)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i128)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i128::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(four), two.checked_mul(two));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_mul(two));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Return [`NonZero::<i128>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2i128)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i128)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i128::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(four, two.saturating_mul(two));
assert_eq!(max, four.saturating_mul(max));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Multiplies two non-zero integers together, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self * rhs > i128::MAX`, or `self * rhs < i128::MIN`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let two = NonZero::new(2i128)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4i128)?;

assert_eq!(four, unsafe { two.unchecked_mul(two) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

Raises non-zero value to an integer power. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i128)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i128)?;
let half_max = NonZero::new(i128::MAX / 2)?;

assert_eq!(Some(twenty_seven), three.checked_pow(3));
assert_eq!(None, half_max.checked_pow(3));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

Raise non-zero value to an integer power. Return [`NonZero::<i128>::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MIN") or [`NonZero::<i128>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3i128)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27i128)?;
let max = NonZero::new(i128::MAX)?;

```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i128>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i128>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2556-2566)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<i128>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<i128>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

- `0-9`

The smallest value that can be represented by this non-zero integer type, equal to [`isize::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.isize.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant isize::MIN").

Shifts the bits to the left by a specified amount, `n`, wrapping the truncated bits to the end of the resulting integer.

Please note this isn’t the same operation as the `<<` shifting operator!

##### Examples

``` rust
let m = NonZero::new(0x6e10aa)?;

```

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Reverses the order of bits in the integer. The least significant bit becomes the most significant bit, second least-significant bit becomes second most-significant bit, etc.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
let n = NonZero::new(0x1234567890123456isize)?;
let m = n.reverse_bits();

assert_eq!(m, NonZero::new(0x6a2c48091e6a2c48)?);
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_bitwise` [\#128281](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/128281))

Converts an integer from big endian to the target’s endianness.

On big endian this is a no-op. On little endian the bytes are swapped.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_bitwise)]
use std::num::NonZeroIsize;
let n = NonZero::new(0x1Aisize)?;

if cfg!(target_endian = "big") {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroIsize::from_be(n), n)
} else {
    assert_eq!(NonZeroIsize::from_be(n), n.swap_bytes())
}
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

##### Example

``` rust
let u_pos = NonZero::new(1usize)?;
let i_pos = NonZero::new(1isize)?;
let i_neg = NonZero::new(-1isize)?;
let i_min = NonZero::new(isize::MIN)?;
let u_max = NonZero::new(usize::MAX / 2 + 1)?;

assert_eq!(u_pos, i_pos.unsigned_abs());
assert_eq!(u_pos, i_neg.unsigned_abs());
assert_eq!(u_max, i_min.unsigned_abs());
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Returns `true` if `self` is positive and `false` if the number is negative.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5isize)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5isize)?;

assert!(pos_five.is_positive());
assert!(!neg_five.is_positive());
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Returns `true` if `self` is negative and `false` if the number is positive.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5isize)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5isize)?;

assert!(neg_five.is_negative());
assert!(!pos_five.is_negative());
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Checked negation. Computes `-self`, returning `None` if `self == NonZero::<isize>::MIN`.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5isize)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5isize)?;
let min = NonZero::new(isize::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.checked_neg(), Some(neg_five));
assert_eq!(min.checked_neg(), None);
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Negates self, overflowing if this is equal to the minimum value.

See [`isize::overflowing_neg`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.isize.html#method.overflowing_neg "method isize::overflowing_neg") for documentation on overflow behavior.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5isize)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5isize)?;
let min = NonZero::new(isize::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.overflowing_neg(), (neg_five, false));
assert_eq!(min.overflowing_neg(), (min, true));
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Saturating negation. Computes `-self`, returning [`NonZero::<isize>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") if `self == NonZero::<isize>::MIN` instead of overflowing.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5isize)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5isize)?;
let min = NonZero::new(isize::MIN)?;
let min_plus_one = NonZero::new(isize::MIN + 1)?;
let max = NonZero::new(isize::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.saturating_neg(), neg_five);
assert_eq!(min.saturating_neg(), max);
assert_eq!(max.saturating_neg(), min_plus_one);
```

1.71.0 (const: 1.71.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Wrapping (modular) negation. Computes `-self`, wrapping around at the boundary of the type.

See [`isize::wrapping_neg`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.isize.html#method.wrapping_neg "method isize::wrapping_neg") for documentation on overflow behavior.

##### Example

``` rust
let pos_five = NonZero::new(5isize)?;
let neg_five = NonZero::new(-5isize)?;
let min = NonZero::new(isize::MIN)?;

assert_eq!(pos_five.wrapping_neg(), neg_five);
assert_eq!(min.wrapping_neg(), min);
```

1.87.0 (const: 1.87.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Returns the bit pattern of `self` reinterpreted as an unsigned integer of the same size.

##### Examples

``` rust
let n = NonZero::new(-1isize).unwrap();

assert_eq!(n.cast_unsigned(), NonZero::<usize>::MAX);
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2isize)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4isize)?;
let max = NonZero::new(isize::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(Some(four), two.checked_mul(two));
assert_eq!(None, max.checked_mul(two));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Multiplies two non-zero integers together. Return [`NonZero::<isize>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let two = NonZero::new(2isize)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4isize)?;
let max = NonZero::new(isize::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(four, two.saturating_mul(two));
assert_eq!(max, four.saturating_mul(max));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`nonzero_ops` [\#84186](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84186))

Multiplies two non-zero integers together, assuming overflow cannot occur. Overflow is unchecked, and it is undefined behavior to overflow *even if the result would wrap to a non-zero value*.

##### Safety

This results in undefined behavior when `self * rhs > isize::MAX`, or `self * rhs < isize::MIN`.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(nonzero_ops)]

let two = NonZero::new(2isize)?;
let four = NonZero::new(4isize)?;

assert_eq!(four, unsafe { two.unchecked_mul(two) });
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Raises non-zero value to an integer power. Checks for overflow and returns [`None`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/option/enum.Option.html#variant.None "variant std::option::Option::None") on overflow. As a consequence, the result cannot wrap to zero.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3isize)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27isize)?;
let half_max = NonZero::new(isize::MAX / 2)?;

assert_eq!(Some(twenty_seven), three.checked_pow(3));
assert_eq!(None, half_max.checked_pow(3));
```

1.64.0 (const: 1.64.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Raise non-zero value to an integer power. Return [`NonZero::<isize>::MIN`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MIN "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MIN") or [`NonZero::<isize>::MAX`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/num/struct.NonZero.html#associatedconstant.MAX "associated constant std::num::NonZero::MAX") on overflow.

##### Examples

``` rust
let three = NonZero::new(3isize)?;
let twenty_seven = NonZero::new(27isize)?;
let max = NonZero::new(isize::MAX)?;

assert_eq!(twenty_seven, three.saturating_pow(3));
assert_eq!(max, max.saturating_pow(3));
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with decimal digits.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<isize>::from_ascii(b"+10"), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<isize>::from_ascii(b"1 ").is_err());
```

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

🔬This is a nightly-only experimental API. (`int_from_ascii` [\#134821](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134821))

Parses a non-zero integer from an ASCII-byte slice with digits in a given base.

The characters are expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert_eq!(NonZero::<isize>::from_ascii_radix(b"A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
#![feature(int_from_ascii)]

assert!(NonZero::<isize>::from_ascii_radix(b"1 ", 10).is_err());
```

1.98.0 (const: 1.98.0) · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2595-2605)

Parses a non-zero integer from a string slice with digits in a given base.

The string is expected to be an optional `+` or `-` sign followed by only digits. Leading and trailing non-digit characters (including whitespace) represent an error. Underscores (which are accepted in Rust literals) also represent an error.

Digits are a subset of these characters, depending on `radix`:

- `0-9`
- `a-z`
- `A-Z`

##### Panics

This method panics if `radix` is not in the range from 2 to 36.

##### Examples

``` rust
assert_eq!(NonZero::<isize>::from_str_radix("A", 16), Ok(NonZero::new(10)?));
```

Trailing space returns error:

``` rust
assert!(NonZero::<isize>::from_str_radix("1 ", 10).is_err());
```

1.35.0 · [Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)

[Source](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/core/num/nonzero.rs.html#2409-2419)
